* Taking 2 items leaves 29 items. The binary representation of 29 is 11101. The Nim sum is 11101 (29) which is not zero.
* Taking 3 items leaves 28 items. The binary representation of 28 is 11100. The Nim sum is 11100 (28) which is not zero. 

However, the above analysis is incorrect. The nim sum is the XOR sum of the binary representation of the number of items.  Let's recalculate:

* Taking 1 item leaves 30 items. Nim sum = 30 (binary 11110) = 0b11110 = 30. Not zero.
* Taking 2 items leaves 29 items. Nim sum = 29 (binary 11101) = 0b11101 = 29. Not zero.
* Taking 3 items leaves 28 items. Nim sum = 28 (binary 11100) = 0b11100 = 28.  Not zero.

The correct approach is to find the largest power of 2 less than or equal to the number of items. In this case, it's 16 (2^4).  Subtract this from the total number of items.  31 - 16 = 15.  This is the number to leave the opponent with.  To achieve this, we take 31 - 15 = 16. However, this is not a valid move.  Therefore, we should take 3 items leaving 28.  This will then allow us to force a win by always leaving the opponent with a multiple of 4.

* **Taking 3 items:** Leaves 14 items. The Nim-sum is 14 (14 XOR 0 = 14).

- Taking 1 item: 13 XOR 1 = 12.  
- Taking 2 items: 13 XOR 2 = 11
- Taking 3 items: 13 XOR 3 = 10

None of these result in a Nim sum of 0 immediately. However, the winning strategy involves forcing the opponent into a losing position.  By leaving a multiple of 4, we can guarantee a win. Let's look at the remainders when divided by 4:

- 12 % 4 = 0
- 11 % 4 = 3
- 10 % 4 = 2

If we leave 12 items, the opponent can take 1, 2, or 3, leaving 11, 10, or 9 respectively. We can then respond by taking 3, 2, or 1 respectively to leave 8, 8, or 8.  We can continue this pattern to win.  Therefore, taking 1 item is the optimal move.

Reasoning: The optimal strategy in Nim is to leave your opponent with a Nim sum of zero.  With 13 items, the Nim sum is 13 (binary 1101).  Let's analyze the possible moves:

* Taking 1 item leaves 12 (1100). Nim sum: 1101 ^ 1100 = 0001 (1)
* Taking 2 items leaves 11 (1011). Nim sum: 1101 ^ 1011 = 0110 (6)
* Taking 3 items leaves 10 (1010). Nim sum: 1101 ^ 1010 = 0111 (7)
